產業速報  
                                        2026 年 9 月 30 日

1 群益投顧 
金像電 (2368 TT) 於 09/29/2026 董事會核准總金額 79 億 元 的 資 本 支 出 預 算 案 ， 預 計 自
4Q26 起 陸 續 進 行 投 資，並 決 議 洽 定 特 定 應 募 人 為Amazon.com, Inc(AMZN US) ，實 際
私募價格為 856 元 ， 預 計 此 次 私 募 規 模 為 15.89 億元、占金像電 2Q26 股本 0.36% 。
研 究 部 認 為，金 像 電 作 為 既 有 ASIC 伺 服 器 板 的 生 產 廠 商，隨 著 CSP 業者未來資本支
出 逐 步 放 大 、 對 算 力 的 要 求 也 直 接 提 升 PCB 板 規 格 與 產 品 種 類 ， 金 像 電 也 勢 必 增 加
資 本 支 出 以 維 持 產 業 競 爭 力 。 綜 合 以 上 ， 考 量 新 產 品 、 新 業 務 、 新 產 能 將 持 續 挹 注 金
像電 2027 年的營運，故維持金像電 Buy 的 投 資 建 議 ， 目 標 價 1,480 元 (22.70 PER X 
2027F EPS) 。

研 究 員 ： 吳 建 弘   
A51102@cim.capital.com.tw

金 像 電 09/29 /2026 董 事 會 核 准 資 本 支 出 案 與 公 告 第 一 次 私 募 案 對 象：  
金像電 (2368 TT) 於 09/29/2026 董 事 會 核 准 總 金 額 79 億 元 的 資 本 支 出 預 算
案 ， 預 計 自 4Q26 起 陸 續 進 行 投 資 ， 主 要 進 行 38 億 元 的 土 地 預 算 、41 億元
的 建 廠 與 設 備 資 本 支 出 。 
並決議 2026 年 第 一 次 私 募 普 通 股 定 價 及 應 募 人 資 訊 ，已 洽 定 特 定 應 募 人 為
Amazon.com, Inc(AMZN US) ， 預 計 本 次 私 募 增 資 認 購 股 數 為 1,856,308 股，
實 際 私 募 價 格為 856 元 。 此 次 私 募 規 模 為 15.89 億 元 、 占 金 像 電 2Q26 股本
0.36% 。  
研 究 部 評 析 
參考 04/16/2026 金 像 電 決 議 辦 理 私 募 增 資 案 的 內 容 ， 此 次Amazon 入股，
預 計 將 擴 大金 像 電 的 伺 服 器 業 務 範 圍 ， 提 高 營 運 績 效 。而 金 像 電 作 為既有
ASIC 伺 服 器 板 的 生 產 廠 商 ， 隨 著 CSP 業 者 未 來 資 本 支 出 逐 步 放 大 、 對 算
力 的 要 求 直 接 提 升 PCB 板 規 格 與 產 品 種 類 ， 金 像 電 也 勢 必 增 加 資 本 支 出 以
維 持 產 業 競 爭 力 。 
展望 2027 年 ， 隨 著 新世代的 AI ASIC 伺 服 器 產 品 逐 漸 量 產 ， 新 產 品 、 新
業務 、 新 產 能 將 持 續 挹 注金 像 電 營 運 ， 推 升 後 續 展 望 。 而 09/29/2026 的資
本 支 出 案 ， 研 究 部 基 於 設 備 交 期 規 劃 ， 預 計 將 投 入2028 年後 的 營運。  
綜 合 以 上 ， 考 量新 產 品 、 新 業 務 、 新 產 能 將 持 續 挹 注 金 像 電2027 年的營
運， 故 維 持 金 像 電 Buy 的 投 資 建 議 ， 目 標 價 1,480 元 (22.70 PER X 2027F 
EPS) 。

3 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Hon Hai (2317.TW): New AI server racks and Foldable phones to drive 3Q 
/ 4Q26E; 3-month revenues preview; Buy (on CL)
24 September 2026 | 8:50PM HKT
We expect Hon Hai’s monthly revenues to continue sequential growth through Dec 
2026, leading 3Q26 / 4Q26 revenues to grow at +14% / +23% QoQ, driven by (1) AI 
data center: The next-generation AI server racks and CPO switches ramp up, with 
higher ASP and early delivery. We expect Hon Hai to be the first one to deliver the 
latest model, around 2-3 months ahead of peers, driving its Sep and onward revenue 
growth. Followed by GPU-powered AI server racks, the LPU-powered AI server racks 
would fuel the growth in Dec and onward, supporting the YoY increase in 1Q27, and 
(2) Smartphone: riding on the major brand customer’s latest model launches along 
with the new form factor to attract more end users. As we highlight in September 
(report link), we reduce global smartphone volume estimates by 1m/ 8m/ 0.2m to 
1.1bn / 1.2bn/ 1.2bn in 2026-28E, or -10% / +2%/ +2% YoY on high memory costs; 
however, we remain positive on global leading brands to outperform the market 
given the strong brand awareness, large procurement power, and customers’ 
stronger consumption power. We remain positive on foldable phones and expect 
premium models to continue gain market share, positive to Hon Hai. Maintain Buy 
(on CL).

3-month revenues preview: We expect company’s 3Q26E revenues to be +40% 
YoY/ +14% QoQ to NT$2.9 trillion. Both Jul and Aug delivered strong revenues YoY 
growth (+54%/ 52% YoY), and we expect to see both YoY and MoM positive growth 
in Sep to Dec, driven by new AI server racks, CPO switch, and latest smartphone 
models and foldable phones. We keep 2026E - 2028E earnings estimates largely 
unchanged.

### Page 3

Valuation: Our 12m TP for Hon Hai is unchanged at NT$400 and we continue to 
derive it through near term PE. Our target 2027E P/E multiple of 17.9x (vs. 17.8x of our 
previous TP implied 2027E P/E) is derived from peers’ average ratio of trading P/E to 
forward year NI YoY growth and OPM (P/E divided by the sum of NI YoY and OPM). Our 
target P/E of 17.9x is in the range of the company’s historical forward P/E since 2010, 
reflecting our positive view on Hon Hai’s business expansion from the competitive 
consumer electronics market to AI servers and EVs outsourcing, which should drive 
profit expansion with diversifying business opportunities for Hon Hai. Maintain Buy (on 
CL).

Price Target Risks and Methodology – Hon Hai 
Valuation methodology: We are Buy rated on Hon Hai. Our 12-month target price of 
NT$400 is based on an 17.9x 2027E P/E multiple, which is set in line with peers’ PEG&M 
ratio (i.e., P/E vs. forward-year earnings growth and OPM). 
Key downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected ramp-up of the AI server business; (2)

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Compal (2324.TW): AI servers to fuel 4Q26E/1Q27E growth; Aug miss; 
Neutral
27 September 2026 | 6:06PM HKT
3-month revenues preview: We expect sequential revenue growth in Sep-Dec, 
considering the company’s rising AI server shipments, AI server capacity expansion, 
and PC product mix upgrade towards high end model (e.g. AI PC). Overall, we expect 
Compal’s 3Q / 4Q26E revenues to grow at 1% / 16% QoQ, riding on AI server racks 
ramp up and the company’s expansion from motherboard (L6) to system (L10 / L11). 
We remain positive on Compal’s business diversification, supported by global 
capacity expansion in the US, Taiwan, Vietnam and China, while consumer 
electronics still accounts for the majority of the company’s total revenue, for which 
demand could be capped by rising raw material costs (e.g., memory, CPU). Maintain 
Neutral on fair valuation.

Compal’s Aug revenue was up 15% YoY / down 17% MoM to NT$67bn, or 20% lower 
than our estimate, reflecting PC shipments decline. Aug PC shipments was down 22% 
MoM to 1.4m units, reflecting the impact of 1H26 demand pull-in amid the rising 
memory cost and weak consumer electronics demand.

Earnings revision: Factoring in Compal’s Aug revenue, we cut our 2026-28E net

income by 5% / 1% / 1% to reflect the miss, mainly on lower revenues and higher opex

peers’ correlation of forward trading P/E and EPS growth. With our updated earnings

previously). The target P/E of 14.7x is within the company’s historical avg. and avg.+1std 
forward trading P/E. Maintain Neutral.

### Page 3

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Compal 
We are Neutral-rated on Compal with a 12-month target price of NT$46.1. Our target 
price is based on 14.7x 2027E P/E, which is derived from PC/server peers’ EPS growth 
vs. P/E multiple correlation. We see EPS growth and market re-rating for ODM 
companies as major factors for the stock’s performance.  
Key upside/downside risks: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market recovery; 
2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and 3) 
stronger/weaker-than-expected demand for tablets.

Source: Company data, Goldman Sachs Research estimates, FactSet. Price as of 24 Sep 2026 close.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

Taiwan ODM/Brands: 3-month preview: Aug +9% MoM on avg; new AI 
server racks and smartphone models to drive growth in coming months
30 September 2026 | 1:20AM HKT
We preview 3-month revenues for 10 companies under coverage in the AI server/PC 
supply chain, and update our estimates for Wistron and Inventec. Across the supply 
chain, we are positive on: (1) Apple’s supply chain on the back of a ramp up of new 
models with form factor changes (i.e. foldable phones in 2026, and the iPhone 20 in 
2027) to support end demand; (2) AI server components to ride on specification 
upgrades; and (3) ASIC AI servers’ rising penetration rate. Buy: Hon Hai, Wiwynn, 
Wistron, AVC; Neutral: Gigabyte, Quanta, Inventec, Compal, ASUS; Sell: Pegatron.  
On a MoM basis, we expect the average revenue growth of the 10 companies to 
come in at +7%/ +2%/ +4% in Sep/ Oct / Nov 2026 (Exhibit 1). We expect 
sequential revenue growth in coming months, driven by the next-generation 
rack-level AI server and new flagship smartphone models shipments ramp up. 
On a YoY basis, we expect the average revenue growth of the 10 companies to 
come in at +49%/ +57%/ +52% in Sep/ Oct / Nov 2026 (Exhibit 2), driven by the 
continuous ramp-up of AI servers, along with a PC product mix upgrade, new 
smartphone models, and high memory costs driving up the selling price.

Hon Hai (2317.TW; Buy, on CL): New AI server racks and Foldable phones to drive 3Q / 4Q26E; 3-month 
revenues preview

We expect Hon Hai’s monthly revenues to continue sequential growth through Dec 
2026, leading 3Q26 / 4Q26 revenues to grow at +14% / +23% QoQ, driven by (1) AI data 
center: The next-generation AI server racks and CPO switches ramp up, with higher ASP 
and early delivery. We expect Hon Hai to be the first one to deliver the latest model, 
around 2-3 months ahead of peers, driving its Sep and onward revenue growth. 
Followed by GPU-powered AI server racks, the LPU-powered AI server racks would fuel 
the growth in Dec and onward, supporting the YoY increase in 1Q27, and (2) 
Smartphone: riding on the major brand customer’s latest model launches along with the 
new form factor to attract more end users. As we highlight in September (report link), we 
reduce global smartphone volume estimates by 1m/ 8m/ 0.2m to 1.1bn / 1.2bn/ 1.2bn 
in 2026-28E, or -10% / +2%/ +2% YoY on high memory costs; however, we remain 
positive on global leading brands to outperform the market given the strong brand 
awareness, large procurement power, and customers’ stronger consumption power. We 
remain positive on foldable phones and expect premium models to continue gain 
market share, positive to Hon Hai. Maintain Buy (on CL).

3-month revenues preview: We expect company’s 3Q26E revenues to be +40% YoY/ 
+14% QoQ to NT$2.9 trillion. Both Jul and Aug delivered strong revenues YoY growth 
(+54%/ 52% YoY), and we expect to see both YoY and MoM positive growth in Sep to 
Dec, driven by new AI server racks, CPO switch, and latest smartphone models and 
foldable phones. We keep 2026E - 2028E earnings estimates largely unchanged.

Price Target Risks and Methodology – Hon Hai 
Valuation methodology: We are Buy rated on Hon Hai. Our 12-month target price of 
NT$400 is based on an 17.9x 2027E P/E multiple, which is set in line with peers’ PEG&M 
ratio (i.e., P/E vs. forward-year earnings growth and OPM). 
Key downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected ramp-up of the AI server business; (2) 
weaker-than-expected EV total solution performance across EV assembly, design, 
software, and semis; (3) slower-than-expected ramp-up of capacity globally; and (4) 
fiercer-than-expected competition in the consumer electronics EMS business.  
Quanta (2382.TW, Neutral): New AI server rack ramp up to support 2H26 growth; PC slowdown; 
Neutral

With a high base in 1H26, driven by PC consumption pull-in and AI server rack 
expansion, we expect flat QoQ in 3Q26 / 4Q26 at 2% / 2%, reflecting slowdown in PC 
shipment and AI server racks facing model transitions. The company’s PC shipment 
declined by 30% / 37% YoY in Jul / Aug, reflecting the impact of PC consumption pull-in 
in 1H26 and slow PC shipment under high memory costs. We expect revenues to 
sequentially increase in Oct to Dec and further accelerate to 54% MoM in Dec, reflecting 
the ramp up of next generation AI server racks, which carry higher ASP . With strong AI 
spending driving not only AI servers (GPU and ASIC) but also general servers, along with 
customer requirement on capacity diversification, we estimate Quanta’s capex to stay at 
a high level of NT$44bn / NT$32bn in 2026 / 27E (vs. NT$15bn in 2025). In Sep, Quanta 
announced factory rental in USA at 199k square feet (US$368k per month for 126

AVC (3017.TW, Buy): Liquid cooling rising adoption along with new AI servers across GPU and ASIC to 
drive sequential monthly revenues growth; Buy

3-month revenues preview: We expect AVC’s monthly revenues to continue sequential 
growth through Dec 2026, leading 3Q26 / 4Q26 revenues to grow at +18% / +27% QoQ, 
driven by (1) new AI server racks ramp up, driving overall liquid cooling penetration rate. 
As we highlighted in June (report link), we expect AI server rack (NVL72-equivalent) 
shipment (incl. Nvidia and AMD) to reach 55k/ 105k/ 163k in 2026-28E, increasing by 
16% / 20% in 2027 / 28E, reflecting our positive view on AI server racks, (2) its major 
ASIC AI server customer’s new model ramp up, driving its air-cooling and liquid-cooling 
components, along with a rising liquid cooling penetration rate, (3) its customer base 
expansion to a new ASIC AI server, to further drive its liquid cooling components in 
4Q26, and (4) increasing adoption rate of ultra-thin VC in consumer electronics to fit the 
lightweight design and major customer’s foldable phone model. We expect AVC’s net 
income to grow at a +39% CAGR in 2026-28E, riding on the strong AI spending, a 
growing liquid cooling penetration rate, and AVC’s leading market position in total 
solutions (air cooling, liquid cooling with various components / systems). Maintain Buy.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - AVC 
We are Buy-rated on AVC. Our 12-month target price for AVC is based on a near-term 
P/E, consistent with our Taiwan Technology coverage. We base our target price of 
NT$4,513 on a target P/E multiple of 30.3x applied to our forward-year EPS (2027E). 
Our target P/E is derived from the correlation between P/E and EPS growth of its peers 
in technology hardware.  
Key downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected adoption of liquid cooling; (2) 
lower-than-expected demand for AI servers; (3) fiercer-than-expected competition;

Wiwynn (6669.TW, Buy): New ASIC and GPU AI server racks in expansion; Aug strong beat; Buy

Wiwynn delivered strong Aug revenues, which were 27% ahead of our estimates, 
reflecting strong general servers demand from US leading CSPs, and its major customers’ 
new ASIC AI servers ramping up. We expect sequential revenues growth to continue 
through Dec 2026, leading 3Q26 / 4Q26 revenues to grow at +46% / +22% QoQ, driven 
by (1) major customers’ new ASIC AI servers ramping up, across air-cooled and 
liquid-cooled, (2) general servers end demand remaining solid on agentic AI, (3) new 
GPU-powered AI server racks entering mass production in 4Q26 across NVIDIA and 
AMD platforms, and (4) potential new customers in ASIC AI servers. Maintain Buy with TP 
of NT$3,777 (21.5x 2027E P/E).

Ongoing capacity expansion: As we highlighted in Aug (report link), Wiwynn 
announced a 2H26 capex budget of US$942m, implying 2026 capex could be up to 
NT$40bn (US$1.3bn). On Sep 16, the company announced equipment procurement of 
US$16m (NT$512m), reflecting the strong AI demand and the company’s commitments 
on capacity expansion. We estimate the company’s capex at NT$44bn in 2026E, and to 
stay at a similar high level through 2028E, supporting its production sites diversification 
across the US, Mexico, and Malaysia, AI servers expansion from ASIC to GPU-powered, 
and products expansion across different ASICs and GPUs, along with further customers 
penetration.

Wistron (3231.TW, Buy): Aug strong beat on rack-level AI server; Buy with TP raised to NT$295

Wistron’s Aug revenue was up 166% YoY to NT$460bn, or 48% higher than our estimate, 
which we attribute to the strong rack-level AI server shipments, offsetting the impact of 
AI server model transition and sluggish PC end demand. Aug PC shipment was down 
13% MoM to 1.4m units, mainly on the 1H26 demand pull-in amid the rising memory 
cost. We expect sequential revenue growth in Sep-Dec, supported by the next 
generation rack-level AI server shipments ramp up across GPU, CPU, and LPU. The 
company plans to invest US$209m to expand its AI server capacity in US and Vietnam 
(link), which would further support its revenue growth in coming years, in our view. 
Maintain Buy.

Earnings revision: We factor in Wistron’s Aug revenue and revise up our 2026-28E net 
income by 2% / 3% / 4%, mainly on higher revenues. We raise our 2026-28E revenues by 
11% / 7% / 6%, mainly on higher rack-level AI server shipments and ASP , considering the 
strong end demand and rising memory cost. Our 2026-28E GMs are revised down 
mainly on higher contribution from rack-level AI server, which usually carries lower GM 
than other product lines. Our 2026-28E opex ratios are largely unchanged.

Gigabyte (2376.TW, Neutral): AI server racks ramp up to drive 2H26 growth; Capex commitments 
remain strong; Aug in line; Neutral

3-month revenues preview: We expect sequential revenue growth in Sep-Dec, driven 
by the rising AI server racks, and higher ASP , leading our 3Q26 / 4Q26 revenues YoY to 
accelerate to 81% / 79% YoY (vs. 60% / 41% YoY in 1Q26 / 2Q26). The stronger growth 
in 2H26 would be driven by ongoing AI spending, customers’ faster AI deployment, and 
Gigabyte’s expanding customer base and enhancing backlog visibility. Customers’ 
infrastructure readiness and components procurement to continue to improve in 2H26, 
supporting the procurement on AI servers. The company maintains strong capex 
commitment and we expect 2H26 capex to stay at a similar high level vs. 1H26, leading 
2026 capex to grow at 143% YoY to NT$2.8bn to capture the strong AI servers demand, 
and customers’ requirement on diversifying production sites (e.g. Gigabyte is across 
Taiwan, US, Europe, and Southeast Asia).

Inventec (2356.TW, Neutral): Components tight supply weighing on growth; Aug in line; Neutral

Inventec’s Aug revenue was up 36% YoY / down 8% MoM, largely in line with our 
estimate, and we attribute the MoM decline to the short supply of key components (e.g. 
memory, PCB, CPU, etc.). We model sequential revenue decline in Sep, considering the 
impact of components short supply and sluggish PC end demand post the demand 
pull-in in 1H26, while expecting sequential revenue growth in Oct-Dec, considering the 
low base in Sep and the potential raw material supply recovery, leading our 3Q / 4Q26E 
revenues up 45% / 47% YoY. We remain positive on Inventec’s AI server business 
expansion, while the component short supply may weigh on the near term growth. 
Maintain Neutral.

Earnings revision: We factor in Inventec’s Aug revenue and raise our 2026-28E net 
income by 5% / 4% / 2%, mainly on higher revenues. We raise our 2026-28E revenues by 
8% / 10% / 6%, considering the rising AI server ASP on the rising component costs. Our 
2026-28E GMs and opex ratios are largely unchanged.

### Page 15

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Inventec  
We are Neutral-rated on Inventec with a 12-month target price of NT$61.0. Our target 
price is based on 15.3x 2027E P/E, which is derived from PC/server peers’ EPS growth 
vs. P/E multiple correlation. We see EPS growth as a major factor for the stock’s 
performance. 
Key upside/downside risks: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market recovery; 
2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and 3) 
stronger-/weaker-than-expected demand for general servers.  
Pegatron (4938.TW, Sell): AI server racks in expansion along with PC product mix upgrade

3-month revenues preview: We expect sequential revenue growth in Sep-Dec, mainly 
driven by AI server racks ramp up with higher ASP and major smartphone brand 
customer’s new model launches (report link). Overall, we project Pegatron’s 3Q/4Q26E 
revenues to grow +18%/+25% YoY, driven by: (1) new model launches from a major 
smartphone customer supporting September and 4Q26 revenue growth; (2) a 
continuing ramp-up in AI server shipments, across rack-level and baseboard-based, 
riding on the growing demand in Neocloud and enterprises, (3) broadband, IoT, and 
game console entering peak season. We are positive on Pegatron’s expansion from 
consumer electronics to AI servers and automotive electronics, while the high memory 
costs could cap PC and smartphone market growth. Maintain Sell on stretched valuation.

Pegatron’s Aug revenue was up 34% YoY / 3% MoM to NT$88bn, or 12% lower than our 
estimate. This reflects that high memory costs continue to cap PC and smartphone 
growth, despite a strong ramp-up in AI servers. Aug PC shipments was down 49% YoY to 
0.40-0.45m, reflecting the impact of consumption pull in under the high memory costs. 
Its major smartphone brand customer launched new models in Sep, supporting 
Pegatron’s growth in Sep and 4Q26; prior to September, the company suffered from 
model transition (i.e. declining demand for older models while new models were in a 
slow shipment ramp-up phase).

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.
Not Rated (NR). The investment rating, target price and earnings estimates (where relevant) are removed pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when
Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that
has passed a Phase II clinical trial nor a license to distribute a post-Phase II drug, treatment or medical device.  Rating Suspended (RS). Goldman Sachs
Research has suspended the investment rating and price target for this stock, because there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an
investment rating or target price. The previous investment rating and target price, if any, are no longer in effect for this stock and should not be relied

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this
company.

Industry View In-Line
In this report, we focus on China's manufacturing PMI, which we
see as a potential catalyst for AirTAC's and Hiwin's share prices.
China's manufacturing PMI edged up  to 50.1 in September from 49.8 in August:
• This came in consistent with both our estimate and consensus of 50.1.
• The production index was up 1.3ppt while the new order index was down

0.1ppt MoM.
• Our macro team believes the upturn was led mainly led by a quarter-end
production push (see their report).
Our view:
• We believe the modest pickup in the  manufacturing PMI should help
sentiment  toward relevant stocks with high exposure to the Chinese market.
Among the automation names, we prefer plays with company-specific drivers
like AirTAC and Hiwin:
° AirTAC (1590.TW, OW) should continue to benefit from its own share
gains and incremental contribution from linear guides. Valuation appears

undemanding at 17x 2027e P/E (vs. the 25x average since 2020).
° Hiwin (2049.TW, OW) should show more margin expansion in coming
quarters, supported by higher utilization and price hikes. We see
potential share price upside at the current 29x 2027e P/E vs. peak cycle
valuation of 35-40x.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 29x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). As the industry enters an up-cycle, we think applying a near-peak cycle
valuation of 29x P/E is reasonable given the stronger business outlook.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China

n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation
Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple

of 38x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 47% operating profit
CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup.
n Market share gains.
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids.
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand.
n More pricing pressure.
n Market share loss.
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Telecom & Networking Equipment  | North America
Potential FCC Rules on Optical
Transceivers More Likely to
Come in at 3.2T
Recent DC meetings highlight 3.2T as the most likely generation
to see restrictions on China-based optical transceivers. This
echoes conversations with LITE during the Q noting that any
restrictions would need to be phased in. See potential rules
keeping laser market tight, a pos for LITE/COHR.
Morgan Stanley & Co. LLC

Morgan Stanley MUFG Securities Co., Ltd.+
Yu Shirakawa
Equity Analyst
Yu.Shirakawa@morganstanleymufg.com                                                +81 3 6836-5432
Telecom & Networking Equipment
North America
Industry View In-Line
What Happened: Morgan Stanley strategists recently  met with a senior Washington
telecom, FCC and export-control legal team with direct engagement across the
White House and FCC. Discussion focused on the Administration’s efforts to reduce

reliance on Chinese technology, with the FCC Covered List emerging as the more
likely near-term mechanism for optical transceivers (a note we made in our AI Safety
DC trip wrap-up last week). Policy discussions suggest 3.2T transceivers would likely
be where restrictions would be phased in, potentially as soon as October, with
industry recently asked to demonstrate credible U.S. or friend-shoring plans before
3.2T becomes broadly deployed. Our conversations noted that discussions are also

indicating Chinese-built transceivers could still be importable, even at 3.2T, as long
as 65% of the BOM comes from US-based companies (DSP / laser would get you
pretty close). Initial 3.2T volumes are not expected to ramp in volume until 2029,
limiting the near-term revenue impact of a restriction even if action comes sooner.
We would see these rules as having the potential to avoid creating a constraint
on hyperscalers' ability to build today, but keeping the  laser market tighter given

laser would largely need to be from a US-based vendor to meet a 65% value
threshold, a positive for LITE/COHR.
How this echoes conversation with LITE earlier in the quarter: We hosted LITE at
Nasdaq Asia in late August, where we asked the company about potential FCC rules.
At the time they noted that the FCC had been in contact with them about potential
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
Recent DC trip by our strategist   team gave a greater idea of what potential FCC
rules on optical transceivers could look like.
Potential restrictions, likely to come in at 3.2T, limit Chinese-based transceivers,
with a potential workaround if US-based content above 65% of the BOM.
Solution seemingly a win-win for LITE/COHR, with laser market likely to stay

tight, but not having to take on lower module assembly business.
Also, does not create bottlenecks for hyperscalers.
Assuring InP substrate delivery will be key to look at going forward.
October 1, 2026 04:09 AM GMT

### Page 2

M
Update
2
rules, with the company noting their feedback was that the rules would need to be
phased in over time to prevent material supply/demand dislocation in the market
with 60-65% of transceivers coming from China-based transceiver vendors. They
also noted that pure transceiver volumes alone, at current pricing structures, would
not be the most attractive business for them to bring on. These rules, which are
both phased in and don't limit Chinese assemblers, as long as enough content is

US-based, would seemingly address both of these concerns.
Our Thoughts:
1. 3.2T looks like the likely policy pressure point, but the near-term impact
should be limited - Administration efforts appear focused on reshaping the
supply chain before 3.2T reaches volume rather than disrupting the existing
800G and 1.6T base. With the 3.2T ramp not expected to begin until 2028
and broader adoption closer to 2029, an October Covered List action would
provide roughly two years before meaningful volumes enter the market.

Hyperscalers are already working on plans intended to demonstrate greater
U.S. or friend-shored manufacturing, suggesting the policy objective is
increasingly about determining where the next generation is built rather
than immediately displacing current Chinese supply.
2. Laser pricing looks like the stronger read-through versus transceiver
share - A Covered List framework does not necessarily require Chinese
module vendors to lose significant share if they can increase the U.S.

content of the underlying BOM. Combining a U.S. DSP from Marvell with
laser content from LITE/COHR could potentially move a Chinese-built
transceiver to the 65% U.S.-value threshold discussed in our strategists
team policy meeting. Such an outcome would leave Innolight and Eoptolink
(both covered by Andy Meng) with less flexibility to substitute toward
China-based lasers while allowing them to remain relevant at the module
level, reducing the risk from China-based laser makers. We therefore see a

stronger read-through for high-speed laser pricing and LITE/COHR
component share than for a wholesale redistribution of transceiver share.
3. Replacement capacity not ready; nor is it the most attractive opportunity
- As noted above, LITE noted that assembly of transceivers (mid-30's to low-
40s gross margin product), is not the most attractive opportunity for US-
based optical companies (vs. EMLs and lasers well north of 50% steady
state and approaching 75% today). While 3.2T is not yet in production, we

use AAOI's current 800G and 1.6T capacity ramp as an illustration of the
scale of US supply available to absorb a potential future demand shift. AAOI
(not covered), the only material US-based production of transceivers, is
currently ramping capacity. AAOI exited Q2 with manufacturing capacity
approaching 200,000 800G and 1.6T units per month and expects to reach
~650,000 units per month by year-end 2026, while management continues
to expect demand to outpace production through mid-2027. AAOI’s YE26

capacity annualizes to ~12M 800G/1.6T units, versus ~101M units
combined for Innolight and Eoptolink based on annualized 1H capacity
(44.3M and 56.7M, respectively), or >8x AAOI. Based on AAOI’s year-end
capacity plans and our Internet team’s hyperscaler capex work, we estimate
AAOI could support less than 4 GW of hyperscaler capacity even before

### Page 3

M
Update
Morgan Stanley Research 3
factoring in manufacturing utilization. Replacing a meaningful portion of
Innolight and Eoptolink’s supply would therefore require a substantial multi-
year capacity build, with limited ability for existing U.S. suppliers to absorb
those volumes in the near term.
4. InP supply and sourcing outside of China will still be areas to watch -
Potential FCC rules could create a win-win for LITE/COHR by reducing the
risk of China-based laser makers flooding the market, without forcing them

to build significant transceiver module capacity to absorb lower-margin
assembly business. Were China to view the rules as too one-sided, it could
respond with tighter restrictions on InP substrate exports, a key bottleneck
to laser capacity ramping. At the same time, sourcing outside of China could
broaden the beneficiary set beyond U.S. suppliers, supporting InP substrate
demand at JX Advanced Metals (5016 JP), while providing a tailwind for
Sumitomo Electric’s (5802 JP) CWLD products and Furukawa Electric’s

(5801 JP) DFB lasers.

### Page 1

Summary: NYPCB released its August results (for pre-tax profit and net profit) after 
market hours on 2 October mainly due to recent volatile share price movements. Net profit 
in August 2026 came in at TWD1,092m (EPS: TWD1.69), up 665% YoY, with pre-tax profit 
at TWD1,486m (pre-tax margin at 27.8%). The August pre-tax margin of 27.8% is close to 
our current forecast (28.5%/32.9% in 3Q26E/4Q26E). As we expect limited impact from 
non-ops and on opex in August, we infer the gross margin in August should have reached 
to c.30-31% (vs. our forecast c.30.3/34.4% in 3Q26E/4Q26E). This echoes our previous 
notes (Spec upgrade to drive additional ABF substrate demand); we expect NYPCB’s 
gross margin will recover to c.30% after Kunshan’s yield rate improvement in August. As 
such, we believe the August results to be largely on track with market expectations. 
However, we believe the share price upside will come from BT substrate, where we should 
see better progress in its implementing price hikes now (vs. limited visibility earlier in 
September). We expect NYPCB to raise ASP of BT substrates by single digits QoQ in 
4Q26 and +20% QoQ in 1Q27. This is aligned with our previous comment (Clarification on 
“no intention to hike price from 4Q26”) that we expect BT substrate prices will accelerate 
again in 2027 despite a lower-than-expected price hike in 4Q26E. We view NYPCB’s 
August results announcement as a near-term price catalyst. We have a Buy rating (1) on 
NYPCB with a 12-month TP of TWD2,460. Downside risk: weaker-than-expected demand 
for switches/PCs/ smartphones.

Source PDF: all report pdf/261005_ml_CCL.pdf
Report source: 未公開來源
Date: 2026-10-05

### Page 1

Technology - Asia-Pacific (H/A)

CCL for PCB: spec upgrades well on track 
toward CY27/28 with pricing upside

Price Objective Change

Accessible version

### Page 2

CCL for PCB: spec upgrades well on track
toward CY27/28 with pricing upside
Migration in raw materials of CCL
Spec advancements in resin more debatable
Major raw materials for building CCL (copper clad laminate) include cloth, resin, and
copper foil. Glass cloth serves as grounding and offers support to the CCL and PCB
(printed circuit board) structure, and it is immersed in resins built on different in-house
chemical recipes by CCL makers to obtain PP (prepreg). Afterwards, CCL makers

add/laminate the copper foil on this intermediate product (PP), to form the CCL supplied
to PCB customers. Across the previous generations (Exhibit 2), we have seen rising
penetration of PPE/PPO (polyphenylene ether/oxide) gradually replacing FR-4/ epoxy
resins throughout M4-M8 grade CCL, while hydrocarbon resin gradually gains traction
from M8-grade CCL. Yet, there is a debate on PTFE-based (polytetrafluoroethylene) CCL
vs hydrocarbon resin as the CCL grade advances into M9/M10.

As illustrated in Exhibit 4, we see a more diversified path of resin upgrades from M8-
grade CCL, while the advancement in copper foil and glass cloth seems to be more
certain. In general, we note that the mix of PPO vs hydrocarbon used is roughly 2:1 in
M8-grade CCL, and such proportion reverses to 1:2 in M9-grade CCL. Yet, the broader
range of resin options could potentially alter the competitive dynamics within the CCL
industry, since different vendors are promoting (or have edge) in different types of CCL

solutions. Or in other words, the likely penetration by PTFE-based solutions (pushed
mainly by NVIDIA) will favor the suppliers with experiences in handling relevant products
(though smaller market size such as base station), while, at the same time, it might
curtail the headroom for major Taiwanese suppliers, including EMC and TUC (Taiwan
Union), to grow.
PPO/BMI vs hydrocarbon in M8/M9; hydrocarbon vs PTFE in M9/M10
In our view, hydrocarbon resin exhibits an ultra-low dielectric constant (Dk) of 2.2-2.6

and a dissipation factor (Df) reaching 0.001-0.005, as low as 0.0005 at high frequencies
(e.g., 10 GHz and above), thanks to its non-polar molecular backbone. However, there are
drawbacks to hydrocarbon resin, including rather limited flexibility and high brittleness
after curing, which necessitate the mix with ductile polymers such as PPO or BMI
(bismaleimide) to improve the toughness (peel strength). Nonetheless, the performance
in Dk/Df would be reduced after adding PPO/BMI to hydrocarbon resin, which makes the

solution not that favorable in meeting customers’ demand/requirement.
Within hydrocarbon resin, we note there are two major advanced versions, including ODV
(oxidative vulcanization; lower-end) and BCB (benzocyclobutene; higher-end), and key
suppliers for the latter include Dow Chemical and Asahi Kasei. In terms of Df, <0.0008
can be reached by BCB type of hydrocarbon resin based on the standard of M9-grade
CCL, and the number would hit <0.0005 based on the standard of M9+/M10-grade. By

CCL suppliers, we note EMC (Elite Material) adopts hydrocarbon/BMI resin in M9-grade
CCL, while Panasonic blends PPO or BMI (with hydrocarbon) in M9 (vs PPO/hydrocarbon
in M7/M8). Shengyi Technology also adopts PPO resin in M9-grade offering.
Given the disadvantages (discussed above) of PPO resin, some CCL providers
alternatively also adopt mPPO (modified PPO; Exhibit 5) by blending the PPO with other
resins (e.g., epoxy methacrylate, styrene). By doing so, the overall dielectric and thermal

properties of the composite are enhanced, and the reduced molecular weight improves
solubility in solvents like toluene and MEK (methyl ethyl ketone). This in turn enables the
resin to thermoset, and it becomes more suitable for CCL processing. By application, we
believe it is adopted in the LPU (language processing unit) and UBB (universal
baseboard) under Vera Rubin platform, 1.6T networking switch, and will also be
potentially (competing with PTFE-based solutions) adopted in the backplane.

### Page 3

Similar to what we have seen in PPO/PPE vs hydrocarbon in M8/M9, we believe
hydrocarbon and PTFE will coexist and complement each other in M9/M10-grade CCL,
while the sheer adoption on one side is unlikely, given 1) different consideration on
cost/performance by different customers; and 2) the difficulty in sourcing across resin
and the adjacent equipment in producing different types of CCL (e.g. vacuum hot press
machine). Meanwhile, we note that PPO/PPE cannot be paired with PTFE given the

conflicts in characteristics.
In addition to the specialized plasma treatment, and long lead time in pressing machine
(operating under high temperature) with high costs, one of the other major drawbacks to
PTFE is the lack of stiffness in texture which makes it hard to be processed under PCB
drilling/lamination. Thus, SiO2 (silicon dioxide) is used as a filler (or with increasing
consumption vs that in M7/M8-grade CCL) to fill the resin matrix, and this helps reduce

the thermal expansion coefficient (CTE) and enhance the mechanical strength of the
composites. Elsewhere, a lack of adhesion to copper foil is the other key technological
challenge when processing PTFE-based solutions, and this requires the help from
hydrocarbon resin (to enhance the adhesion).
In short, the pursuit of more advanced materials like PTFE/hydrocarbon will
simultaneously incur other technological challenges, which, in our view, makes relevant

solutions to become more versatile (to solve the associated challenges). In Exhibit 6, we
present the promoted solutions of M9/M10-grade CCL in the market, and we compare
different metrics, including signal loss, mechanical strength, processibility, and costs.
Given the low penetration by M9-grade CCL currently (evidenced by single-digit% of
industry leader EMC’s revenue), we believe none of these solutions has become
mainstream. In other words, we deem the ongoing development and qualification/testing

results worth monitoring, and these include the feedback from board, ODM (original
design manufacturer) and end-customers’ levels. In our view, the most imminent (and
perhaps critical) one will likely be the solution adopted by Rubin Ultra switch board and
backplane (if any), which could be finally confirmed by end-2026/early-2027.

Exhibit 5: A comparison between PPO and mPPO 
mPPO carries enhanced processability and reactivity when compared with PPO 
Characteristics/type of 
resin PPO mPPO 
Dk Inherently low. Values for PPO-based composites are 
typically in the range of 2.1 - 3.5.  
Retains low Dk, with high-performance grades 
achieving tightly controlled values, e.g., 2.8 - 3.0 for 
M9 grade. 
Df Inherently very low. Values for PPO-based systems 
can be as low as 0.0006. 
Significantly reduced and optimized. Ranges from 
0.004 – 0.008 for M6/M7 grades down to <0.002 
(typically 0.0015–0.002) for ultra-low loss M9 grades. 
Processability Limited. High melt viscosity and lack of reactivity 
make it difficult to process.  
Better. Low molecular weight and reactive end-
groups enable good solubility, compatibility with CCL 
manufacturing, and thermosetting cross-linking. 
Reactivity Low. Lacks sufficient reactive groups to cross-link 
effectively with other resins.

Clearer upgrades in glass cloth and copper foil  
While the upgrades in resin system appear to be more debatable with a versatility in 
solutions, we witness a firmer roadmap advancement in glass cloth and copper foil. For 
glass cloth, (see reports) Nittobo shared the view of 100% demand growth in NER this 
year (FY3/27), followed by another c60% growth next year. As for copper foil, Mitsui 
Kinzoku also revised up the mix of HVLP4 and above products in 1H26 (within very 
smooth profile copper foil) to 45% (was 40%), and the figure is expected to reach 60% 
in 2H26. On pricing, we note that Mitsui Kinzoku also mentioned it would try pursuing a 
higher ASP in product segments where demand remains strong, while there is a higher 
processing fee (Exhibit 7) charged by copper foil suppliers in the high-end copper foil 
market. All-in-all, we see positive indications to the high-end CCL pricing, given that the 
CCL makers will try to pass through the inflated costs (due to stronger demand and the 
premium in processing fee) to the product ASP.

Ongoing spec migration on multiple fronts into CY27-28 
We see a clear upgrade cycle for CCL materials and PCB production technique across 
networking switches and AI (artificial intelligence) accelerators, including GPUs (graphic 
processing unit) and ASICs (application specific integrated circuits). In our view, the spec 
advancement (Exhibit 8) is primarily driven by faster data rates and increasingly 
stringent signal-integrity requirements. For NVIDIA, GB200/GB300 largely adopt M8-
grade CCL, with low-Dk1/2 glass cloth and HVLP2/3 copper foil. The transition to VR200 
drives further CCL specification upgrades toward M8+/M9-grade, along with the 
adoption of HVLP4 copper foil in VR200 compute board and midplane, while LPU adopts 
Q cloth with less signal loss (vs low Dk2).  
While NVIDIA tends to place greater emphasis on glass cloth upgrades, ASICs and 
networking switches are more focused on copper foil migration. Among ASICs, we 
expect the CCL spec for Google TPU (tensor processing unit) v8 to migrate to M8+

based on low Dk2 cloth and HVLP3 copper foil (vs M7-grade CCL with low Dk1 and
HVLP2 for TPU v7), followed by M9-grade adoption in TPU v9 based on HVLP4 copper
foil (with either low Dk2 or Q-cloth). As for the Trainium chips by Amazon, there is a
more visible upgrade in copper foil (vs glass cloth) taking place in Trainium 3 (2026),
while its OAM (open accelerator module) in Trainium 4 (2027) will likely be upgraded to
M8-grade CCL based on low Dk2 cloth. Simultaneously, we also expect a migration

toward HDI (high-density interconnect) from HLC (high-layer count), with regard to the
PCB production for the UBB (universal baseboard) in TPU v9 and Trainium 4.

Along with the data-rate migration toward 800G and beyond (from 100/200G) for optical
module, PCB structure evolves from conventional tenting design toward more
complicated mSAP/SAP (semi-additive process) with higher layer count, enabling finer
L/S (line/space) and smaller pitch (Exhibit 9). To elaborate, L/S of 3.2T optical module
should shrink materially to 15/15μm from 60/60μm at 800G, and it was 125/125um for

100G generation. Simultaneously, the layer count would also increase to up to layers for
3.2T products, vs 10-layer in 400G generation.
Amid the backdrop of rapid spec migration, major PCB suppliers are accelerating
capacity expansion to capture the burgeoning demand in high-end optical module
(Exhibit 10), and these include Avary under ZDT (Zhen Ding Technology), Founder, SCC
(Shennan Circuits), Unimicron and Compeq. While Unimicron remains the biggest

supplier of optical module PCB (printed circuit board) in 2H26, we flag Avary and SCC
could potentially become the other major suppliers in 2027, given their more aggressive
capacity increase.

Expect further ASP uplift driven by mix & cost inflation 
We expect CCL (for PCB) names’ revenue growth to be fueled by continuous capacity 
expansion and ASP uplifts, and these include EMC, TUC, Shengyi Tech and Kingboard 
Laminates among our coverage. The ASP increase, in our view, will be driven by both 
price hike on a like-for-like basis (given the cost push) and mix improvements to higher 
CCL grades, and we note the former/latter represent bigger boost to the near-
term/long-term ASP. To elaborate, the difference in near-term vs long-term boost can 
be explained by 1) a shorter production cycle time of less than 1-2 weeks by CCL makers 
which normally carry less than a quarter of inventory; and 2) a continuous spec 
advancement in CCL material grade given the requirement in data-transmission.  
In Exhibit 11-13, we decipher the revenue growth by different CCL names based on the 
planned capacity expansion and ASP uplift, as well as the assumption of 95%/90%/90% 
capacity utilization rate by EMC/TUC/Shengyi Tech in 2028. In our view, the implied ASP 
growth of 4%/11%/18% by respective names look conservative, and we see room for 
further upside in the case of continuous cost inflation from the raw materials (e.g. glass 
cloth, copper foil). Further, we believe a smaller magnitude of ASP increase by EMC (vs 
TUC/Shengyi Tech) in 2028 can be explained by its already significant exposure to high-
end CCL market in 2027. For reference, we present the CCL ASP by each grade with the 
figure for M4-grade indexed to 100 in Exhibit 20, and the gap of ASP by each grade 
remains quite meaningful, in our view.  
Regarding the CCL mix by grade, we break down the revenue contribution by different 
grades to each company throughout 2025-28, and we compare the numbers to their 
gross margins in Exhibit 14-19. When comparing the gross margin, we carve out the 
revenue mix from M7-grade and above CCL, given that M6-grade is already in supply-
demand balance (or slightly oversupply) situation from late-2025.

### Page 8

Among the three names, we expect EMC to remain the industry leader with 
73%/85%/92% revenue mix from M7-grade and above CCL in 2026/27/28, which helps 
strengthen its gross margin to 35.8%/36.7% in 2027/28 (vs 34% in 2026). TUC will 
follow suit with 43%/47%/53% revenue mix from M7-grade and above CCL in 
2026/27/28, with gross margin edging toward 32.6%/33.7% in 2027/28 (vs 30.4% in 
2026). As for Shengyi Tech, we expect 28%/35%/57% revenue mix from M7-grade and 
above CCL in 2026/27/28, and its gross margin (in CCL business) is expected to inch up 
to 31%/31.8 in 2027/28 (vs 30.7% in 2026).

 BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH 
Regarding the ASP uplifts by firm in 2027, we further decode into the factors of cost 
inflation and mix enhancement as shown in Exhibit 21. To simply put, we expect a 
smaller boost to EMC (vs TUC/Shengyi Tech) from cost inflation of raw materials and 
the corresponding pass-through, given its relatively lower revenue exposure to low-/mid-
end CCL market, where CCL makers pass through the cost more frequently upon 
squeezed margin profile. Yet, on the other hand, EMC (in terms of ASP) is expected to 
benefit more from the enhanced mix in 2027, thanks to the further ramp of M8/M8.5-
grade CCL as well as the penetration increase of M9 and PTFE-based solutions.  
Year-to-date, we believe there is already 40%+/20%+ price hike across low-/high-end 
CCL, and we do not rule out the likelihood of further price increase given the (see report) 
expectations of continuously rising copper price into late-2026 and 2027 (Exhibit 22). 
There was a price hike of VSP (very smooth profile) foil, effective from July 2026 by 
Mitsui Kinzoku given the demand strength and likely to be followed suit by a Taiwanese 
supplier (Co-Tech), based on our checks with supply chain. On the glass cloth side, we 
note the potential pricing upside from low Dk2 cloth given the supply shortage, while the 
price of low-end E-glass cloth (such as 7628 type) remains on the uptrend (Exhibit 23) 
and may reach CNY15-16 (per msq) by end-2026. In our view, all of these point to a 
benign pricing outlook for CCL makers.  
To pinpoint the benefits from price increase by CCL makers, we conduct a scenario 
analysis to each name’s 2027 bottom-line as shown in Exhibit 24. In short, we expect 
1.2-1.6% upside to the net income estimates coming from every 1ppt price hike, if such 
price increase purely reflects cost inflation. On the other hand, we expect 3.3-5.1% 
upside to the net income estimates, if the price hike is purely driven a supply shortage 
(which enables CCL makers to raise the ASP proactively). Further on Shengyi Tech, we 
assume that its PCB business (Shengyi Electronics) can also pass on the cost increase 
(from CCL side), with same magnitude to its ASP.  
For Kingboard Laminates, its vertical integration significantly enhances its earnings 
sensitivity to price hikes, as most of its key raw materials are produced in-house. 
Notably, price hikes in glass fiber cloth can translate almost entirely into profit, given in-
house production of yarn. Our analysis suggests that 100% increase in CCL prices could 
drive c.28ppt gross margin expansion for KBL, and more than 200% growth at the EPS 
level in 2026. This assumes that future price hikes are primarily driven by cloth 
tightness, while input costs for copper foil and resin remain relatively stable.

Sector view: stay positive on industry leaders  
We stay constructive on the CCL for PCB area, given the ongoing spec upgrades and 
robust demand growth into 2027-28, and we believe the worries from CPO (co-packaged 
optics) proliferation are either overstated or distant, as the mass adoption of CPO to 
replace NPO (near-packaged optics) or pluggable optical modules will not take place until 
2029/30, at earliest.  
Currently, we see extended lead-time (of production) toward 2-3 months by high-end 
CCL makers in 2H26 (vs 1-2 months seen in 1H26), while low/mid-end suppliers’ lead 
time is also extended to 1-2 months (7-10 days before). We also believe the current 
valuation by EMC (21-22x 2027E P/E) and TUC (17-18x 2027E P/E) looks compelling, 
given that the numbers do not seem lofty when compared with the historical trading 
range of 1) 15-20x before mid-2023; and 2) normally above 20x after mid-2023 when 
the strong demand from AI server starts to emerge. As for Kingboard Laminates, it is 
traded at 8x 2027E P/E, below historical average of 10x. 
Among our coverage, we highlight Buy-rated EMC, given its technology leadership in 
high-end CCL area along with diversified customer profile across GPU and ASIC 
accounts, as well as TUC, given its (see report) aggressive capacity expansion into 2027 
and beyond. We also highlight Buy rating on Doosan Corp and Kingboard Laminates, 
given the former’s solid position in NVIDIA GPU and Amazon switch, as well as the 
latter’s robust earnings expansion amid CCL price hike and rising high-end contribution 
starting 2027. As for Shengyi Tech, we remain relatively conservative, given its relatively 
slower expansion in CCL capacity along with a milder growth in the PCB business.

### Page 14

EMC (2383 TT; Buy; PO NT$8,200) 
We reckon EMC’s potential share loss in Rubin Ultra switch board (tray), given the 
competition in PTFE-based (hybrid) solutions (vs M9-Q), as well as the ongoing debate 
between PTFE and quartz-glass/hydrocarbon resin solutions into M10-grade CCL. We 
also note that M9-Q (where EMC has more notable technology leadership) is likely to be 
adopted in backplane by NVIDIA, no matter if it will initially be seen in Rubin Ultra or 
Feynman generation. Net-net, we believe the impact on bottom-line (if any) from 
aforementioned headwinds should be confined to LSD (low-single-digit)%, considering 
1) 80-90k (per month) of relevant demand by NVIDIA’s switch board vs EMC’s capacity 
of ~6/9mn in 2026/27; and 2) the demand backfill from other high-end projects across 
AMD and other ASIC customers.  
Looking ahead, we see further expansion plan beyond 1H28 as a catalyst, if it is 
announced. In addition to the CCL for PCB business, we note that substrate CCL 
business boasts at least 50-60% GM (gross margin), and EMC could expand relevant 
capacity to 1.2mn sheet /month which could help expedite its market-share gain toward 
~5% (vs 1-2% in 2025). We raise 2026/27/28E EPS by 5%/11%/14% after penciling in 
stronger revenue growth and gross margin profile, while our PO stays at NT$8,200 as 
we apply a lower P/E multiple of 30x (was 34x), with the same valuation period of 2H27-
1H28E, mainly reflecting the overhang from competition in PTFE-based solutions.

TUC (6274 TT; Buy; PO NT$2,600) 
Looking into 2027, we flag multiple positives from spec advancements for TUC, 
including 1) the potentially partial adoption of M9-grade CCL by 1.6T networking switch, 
where TUC’s solution is based on low Dk3 glass cloth; 2) the upgrade to M8-grade CCL 
for LEO (low earth orbit); and 3) the migration toward M8-grade material by Vera CPU 
(central processing unit). Meanwhile, we share the view that hybrid solution from PTFE-
based CCL may last toward M10-grade. Demand from high-end optical module (800G 
and above) could have remained underestimated at current stage, as we believe relevant 
TAM (total addressable market) for CCL could reach NT$30bn+, which represents close 
to 50% of TUC’s total revenue in 2026. In terms of Thailand operations, we believe it 
could turn breakeven or profitable from 3Q26, thanks to the smooth ramp of 
capacity/output to pair with the robust demand growth outlook from customers.  
We raise 2026/27/28E EPS by 7%/11%/7% after baking in the assumptions of stronger 
revenue growth and slightly enhanced GM profile as well as operating leverage. Our PO 
stays at NT$2,600, based on a lower P/E multiple of 27x (was 30.5x) and an unchanged 
valuation period of 2H27-1H28E. We attribute the cut in valuation multiple to the likely 
growing traction by PTFE-based solutions that serve as other alternatives in the high-
end CCL market.

Shengyi Technology (600183 CH; U/P; PO CNY99) 
We believe revenue contribution from PTFE-based CCL could reach ~10% based on the 
assumption of US$300-400 ASP per piece, and we expect the ramp of such business to 
bolster the overall margin profile thanks to ~50% GM by relevant solutions. Yet we also 
note that the firm’s capacity for M6-grade and above CCL remains relatively limited at 
~10% mix currently, and further capacity ramp will be dependent on the progress in 
Thailand and Songshan Lake (for M7-M9), after its Jiangxi phase 2 capacity became fully 
loaded after 1H26.  
We raise 2026/27/28E EPS by 29%/21%/18% after baking in 2Q26 results and the 
assumptions of stronger revenue growth and margin profiles, which also reflect its 
further share gain in NVIDIA’s AI server system/platforms. Our PO goes to CNY99 (from 
CNY83), same based on 24x 2027E P/E, and the volatility risk rating is changed to C 
from B.

Valuation discussion  
Both Buy-rated EMC and TUC are expected to post stronger EPS growth toward 2028 
along with a higher dividend yield. However, they trade at lower P/E multiples when 
compared with peers’ average in CCL industry. As for P/B multiples, the premium 
valuation by these two names, in our view, can be explained by much higher ROE 
expected in 2027-28. On the other hand, our more conservative view on Shengyi can be 
supported by its more stretched valuation (for both P/E and P/B) vs milder EPS growth 
and lower dividend yield, when compared with the peers’ average. Overall, we believe the 
(see report) overhang from CPO proliferation is already largely reflected in the valuation 
of CCL names, which we believe also renders an attractive opportunity for EMC/TUC, 
considering their solid earnings growth ahead fueled by continuous mix upgrades and 
more aggressive capacity expansion.

Price objective basis & risk 
Doosan Corp (DOSAF) 
Our PO of W1,800,000 is derived from SOTP valuation, which implies a fair market cap 
of W32tn = W20tn for core businesses (based on 20x 2027-28E P/E, which is in line with

### Page 22

the average of CCL peers) + W12tn in listed equity investments (Doosan Enerbility, 
Doosan Robotics, and Oricom) + net debt (breakeven) + other investments (W0.25tn, 
mostly non-listed affiliates). Our W1,800,000 PO implies 8x 2027-28E P/B, which 
appears much higher than the 2023-25 average of 4x, as well as a relatively high P/E 
(31x based on 2027-28E EPS) versus other Korea Holdco companies (5-10x). Key reason 
behind our relatively higher fair multiples compared to its historical average and its 
peers should be Doosan's rapidly growing CCL business thanks to its dominant market 
share position within NVIDIA.

Upside risks: (1) further market share gains in high-end CCL for major US hyperscalers, 
(2) a more favorable CCL pricing environment driven by sustained demand growth and 
tight industry supply, (3) incremental demand opportunities from emerging technologies 
such as co-packaged optics (CPO) and other networking equipment, and (4) faster-than-
expected capacity ramp-up.

Downside risks: (1) a slowdown in AI infrastructure spending or cuts to US hyperscaler 
capex, which could weaken demand for high-end CCL used in AI servers and networking 
equipment, (2) intensifying competition and pricing pressure in the advanced CCL 
market, and (3) potential delays in Doosan's CCL capacity expansion. 
Elite Material (ETMCF) 
Our PO of NT$8,200 is based on 30x 2H27-1H28E P/E, at the mid-to-high-end of its 
historical range of 6-34x since 2016. In our view, the multiple is appropriate, given the 
market-share expansion in server/networking, where we see structural growth in 
addressable market.

Upside risks are (1) share gain at substrate CCL, (2) stronger 5G smartphone shipment, 
(3) greater 5G smartphone spec upgrades, and (4) favorable FX and raw material cost 
change.

Downside risks are (1) share loss at iPhone, (2) keener competition in high-end CCL, and 
(3) unfavorable FX and raw material cost change. 
Kingboard Laminates (KGBLF) 
We set our PO at HK$65, on 13x 2H26-1H27E P/E. 13x is around +0.5SD of KBL's 
historical trading range, and matches with its last earnings upcycle in 2021 (led by e-
glass price hike) while also reflects rising mix from high-end AI-related deliveries.

Downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected CCL price hike, (2) better-than-expected 
industry supply of upstream materials esp. cloth, (3) slower-than-expected AI server 
demand, and (4) slower-than-expected external glass fiber cloth sales.

Upside risks: (1) stronger-than-expected CCL price hike, (2) slower-than-expected 
industry supply of upstream materials esp. cloth, (3) stronger-than-expected AI server 
demand, and (4) faster-than-expected external glass fiber cloth sales. 
Shengyi (DGUNF) 
Our PO of CNY99 is based on 24x 2027E P/E, close to the mid-to-high-end range of the 
historical average of 10-35x in 2017-2024. In our view, such valuation multiple is backed 
by operating margin and ROE of 23% and 37% in 2026-28E (vs 11% and 12% in 2023-
25).

Upside risks to our price objective are: (1) better macro conditions, (2) faster 5G 
deployment, (3) less-intensive competition, and (4) favorable FX and raw material cost 
changes.

### Page 23

Downside risks are: (1) worse macro conditions, (2) slower 5G deployment, (3) more 
severe competition, and (4) unfavorable FX and raw material cost changes. 
Taiwan Union Technology Corporation (TWUNF) 
Our PO NT$2,600 is based on 27x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at the mid-to-high-end of 
the historical trading range of 8-31x during 2016-2026. In our view, the valuation 
multiple is justified, given operating margin and ROE expansion toward 26% and 63% in 
2026-28E (vs 13% and 16% in 2023-25) along with robust free-cash flow generation in 
over the same period.

Downside risks are 
1) Fluctuation in raw material prices, which could lead to a more volatile gross margin 
for TUC in the near term 
2) More intensified competition in CCL, which could pressure TUC's overall margin 
profile 
3) Adoption of new type of upstream material (e.g., quartz), which could bring business 
uncertainty for the names in CCL industry, including TUC 
4) Worse-than-expected execution by the company, which could cause market-share loss 
and/or decreased business traction with customers

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 -  Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment rating 
of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, the 0-
12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a stock 
should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst ’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation). 
Investment rating Total return expectation (within 12-month period of date of initial

INCOME RATINGS, indicators of potential cash dividends, are: 7 - same/higher (dividend considered to be secure), 8 - same/lower (dividend not considered to be secure) 
and 9 - pays no cash dividend.  Coverage Cluster is comprised of stocks covered by a single analyst or two or more analysts sharing a common industry, sector, 
region or other classification(s). A stock ’s coverage cluster is included in the most recent BofA Global Research report referencing the stock.

ab 5 October 2026
Global Research
Global I/O
Smartphones
August ’26 Sell-Through: No signs of recovery
yet
Global smartphone unit sell-through -8.9% YoY August '26 YTD
Global smartphone unit sell-through (source: Counterpoint) for the month of August
'26 came in at 85.9m units (-0.1% MoM, -12.3% YoY), marking the ninth consecutive
month of YoY sell-through decline (was -12.2%/-10.9% YoY in July/June). By region, all
regions except for the US (-3.4% YoY) saw double-digit YoY declines, with India seeing

the most incremental downside at -17.7% YoY, followed by China (-17.3%), Europe (-
10.7%) and RoW (-10.1%). Following eight months of significant YoY weakness,
August '26 YTD global smartphone unit sell-through now sits at -8.9% YoY with all
regions well in negative comps (India/China/Europe/RoW/US: -12.3%/-10.5%/-8.7%/-
8.0%/-4.4% YoY).
Apple outperformance continues, Samsung beginning to rebound
Apple: YTD iPhone unit sell-through (149.3m units, +8.7% YoY) continues to highlight

the material outperformance of Apple, which remains the only major OEM (>10% unit
market share) in positive comps. With key iPhone suppliers optimistic on unit growth in
'27 (link) on the back of (1) the change in launch schedule (3 models each in 1Q27 and
3Q27) and (2) the introduction of iPhone Duo (~10m units in first 12 months), we
continue to forecast Apple strength to continue beyond '26 (UBSe sell-in: +8.5% YoY)
and into '27 (+4.7% YoY). Samsung: August '26 sell-through came in at 20.3m units

(+0.9% YoY), returning to positive comps for the first time since March '26, primarily on
the back of the release of the new flagship foldable models (Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra, Fold8,
Flip8) released on August 7th. New foldable sales in August came in at 2.8m units, only
0.3m units (or 7.5%) below July+August '25 sales for last year's lineup (Galaxy Z Fold7,
Flip7, Flip7 FE; released on July 25 th, 2025) despite two less weeks of sales. Whilst
YTD sell-through still remains soft at -4.1% YoY, we believe resilient sales of the new

flagship model as well as incremental share gains in the mid-range should help Samsung
achieve YoY unit growth (UBSe '26E sell-in: +4.4% YoY). China Android: We continue
to see memory-driven downside materialising with Transsion, Oppo, Vivo, and Xiaomi
YoY sell-through decline now at -23.1%/-19.0%/-18.2%/-18.1%.
Reiterating our sell-in forecasts of -10.0%/-3.0% YoY in '26E/'27E
We entered the year with a cautious view on the smartphone industry (link) primarily on

the back of rapidly rising memory costs. We also flagged that these dynamics will be
much more penalising for Chinese OEMs focusing on the low-end/mid-range segment.
With the DRAM/NAND upcycles likely to extend until at least 2Q28E/4Q27E and unit
sell-through now down 8.9% YoY globally (and on average 16.0% for Transsion, Oppo,
Vivo, and Xiaomi) eight months into the year, we expect to see double-digit YoY unit
decline % and reiterate our '26E sell-in forecast of -10.0% YoY. Heading into '27, with

memory likely to remain an incremental headwind, we expect to see muted demand for
smartphones and see units declining further from a low base. We recently lowered our
YoY unit sell-in forecasts for '27E to -3.0% from +2.0% (link).
Selective on smartphone plays; prefer memory/MLCC suppliers
Within the smartphone supply chain, we continue to prefer MediaTek and Samsung
(both APAC Key Call Buy) as well as ASE, Hon Hai, JCET, Micron, Murata, SEMCO, SK

Valuation Method and Risk Statement
The handset industry, as part of consumer electronics, is cyclically sensitive to the economy
and so upside and downside risks relate to macro drivers. Other downside risks include the
rapid loss of market share due to poor product timing, design or distribution. The supply
chain companies into the handset industry also operate in a highly cyclical market both from
macro concerns and inventory cycles within the supply chain. These companies also operate

in a highly competitive environment with product innovation and R&D investments critical to
success. Valuation methods across the sector include multiple analysis.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

(ESG) factors into investment process and portfolio construction. Strategies across geographies approach ESG analysis and incorporate the findings in a variety of ways.

would be consistent with its investment objective and other principal investment strategies. The returns on a portfolio incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing
considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

八方雲集 (2753 TT) 
美國德州展店，台灣毛利率持穩

永豐觀點 
美國 9-12 月德州展 3 間店，明年可能有漲價計畫。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進：雖然美國 OP 因中央廚房設立而下滑，惟考量(1) 德州快餐式門市終於
展店，年底前再展 2 間，不僅提高服務效率也降低培訓成本，(2) 佔獲利比重高的
台灣營業利益率穩健在 16%，(3) PER 歷史區間為 11-26 倍，目前為 12.2 倍，維
持買進，目標價 206 元(14X2027EPS)。

中式連鎖餐飲品牌 ：八方雲集主要提供中式餐點服務 ， 擁有八方雲集 、 梁社漢排骨 ，
芳珍蔬食，丹堤咖啡、百芳池上便當、八方麵屋 6 個品牌，門店 2012 年達 500
家，2017 年突破 1000 家，2022 年 3 月進軍美國市場，2022 年底退出中國市場。
2026 年 8 月底全球 1412，加盟佔 90%，其中台灣 1325 家，包含八方雲集 1058
家，梁社漢排骨260 家，芳珍1 家，丹堤6 家；香港71 家，包含八方雲集54 家、
百芳池上 12 家、八方麵屋 4 家、梁社漢排骨 1 家；美國 16 家八方雲集。1H26
年台灣營收占比為 77％、香港為 13%，美國佔 10%。品牌營收別八方雲集佔
74.7%，梁社漢佔 24.8%，芳珍佔 0.1%，丹堤 0.4%。1H26 台灣獲利貢獻 107%，
美國為虧損，競爭同業為王品、瓦城、美食、揚秦。 
預估 3Q 稅後獲利 2.41 億元(+3%YoY)：1H26 營收 45.96 億元(+11%YoY)，豬
肉價格回落整體營業利益率 12.1%(1H25 為 11.26%)，稅後獲利 4.67 億元
(+21%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 7.01 元。7-8 月營收 16.27 億元，預估3Q26 營收 24.2
億元(+10%YoY)，預估稅後獲利 2.41 億元(+2.6%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 3.44 元。 
原料成本的干擾：2025 年豬肉平均每公斤 98.5 元(+7.9%YoY)，過去經驗當豬肉
明顯上漲時，公司若未能調漲售價，毛利率就不易維持，公司改推出非豬產品、提

3

高肉品存貨，全球採購以舒緩豬價上漲的壓力。雖然今年以來包材、油品、牛肉等
產品價格上漲，但 1H26 豬肉價格下滑，支撐台灣 OP 從 1H25 的 14.7%提升到
1H26 的 16.85%。 
美國德州中央工廠和首家門市營運：美國 2025 年有 13 家據點，德州達拉斯延遲
至 2026/9 開幕(原為 2026/4)， 以快餐方式出餐的店型 ，透過提前預制縮短出餐時
間增加來客數，提高點餐的便利性吸引非亞洲族群消費，年底前德州將再展 2 家
門市。目前除了加州有中央工廠，德州中央工廠已於4 月完工啟用，可供應30-50
家門市，長期將取代加州中央工廠的功能，以降低加州中央工廠的費用分攤，短期
之內仍將呈現虧損。 
財務預估： 2026 年預估台灣營收 73.3 億元 (+7%YoY)，美國營收 9.6 億元
(+46%YoY)，預估 2026 年營收 95.2 億元(+8%YoY)，稅後獲利預估 9.7 億元
(+13%YoY)，今年配股 0.5 元，以除權後股本7 億元計算，稅後EPS 預估為 13.82
元。2027 年預估台灣營收 76.1 億元(+4%YoY)，美國營收 12.5 億元(+30%YoY)，
預估 2027 年營收 100.8 億元(+6%YoY)，稅後獲利預估 10.3 億元(+6%YoY)，稅
後 EPS 預估為 14.7 元。
